63. **⚠ A CHARTING BELONGS TO A VISIT, AND THE APP CANNOT SAY WHICH — user, 2026-09-05: *"visit 1 is charting to treatment, same as visit 2"*.** NOT scoped. **Contains a LIVE BUG, measured, that should be fixed first and separately.**
    - **⚠ THE LIVE BUG: the Dental Chart page shows only the FIRST charting of a school year and hides the rest.** `useDentalChartData:85` does `myCharts.find(c => c.iptr_id === iptr._id)` — the first match — and chart creation only fires `if (!chartId)`, so a second charting can never be made from the UI either. **Measured on dev 2026-09-05: 22 of 26 IPTRs have MORE THAN ONE chart.** One pupil has three (2025-08-14, 2026-01-19, 2026-07-09) and the page shows **3 of their 4 tooth records** — the January finding is invisible.
    - **⚠ THE APP CONTRADICTS ITSELF ABOUT THIS.** `tallyIptrServices` deliberately orders MULTIPLE charts per IPTR by date and treats each as a SITTING — that is how the DOH report derives "1st / 2nd application". So the reporting layer assumes several chartings a year while the chart screen assumes one. Both cannot be right.
    - **WHY THE USER'S POINT LANDS HERE:** if Visit 1 and Visit 2 each involve charting, today they collide in one chart and the second overwrites the first, invisibly.
    - **✅ ANSWERED BY THE USER 2026-09-05: THE DENTIST SCREENS AND TREATS AT THE SAME VISIT.** Asked directly ("does she treat at the same visit she screens, or does Visit 1 screen and Visit 2 treat?") — the answer is the first.
      - **So a charting is SELF-CONTAINED: one visit's findings AND the treatments done at that visit, on the same `TOOTH_RECORD` rows.** The model's existing shape — `condition` and `treatment_code` on one row — is therefore CORRECT and needs no change.
      - **⚠ This kills the expensive version of this work.** A tooth does NOT need a history assembled across visits (decayed on chart 1, filled on chart 2, shown together), which would have meant redesigning the chart screen. Each charting stands alone and is read alone.
      - **And it makes the ordinal exact:** one chart per visit means "1st / 2nd application" is read from the visit the chart belongs to, not inferred from dates. That is step 3 below, and it is now a lookup rather than an interpretation.
    - **RECOMMENDED ORDER — three steps, and the first is worth doing alone:**
      1. **Make the chart screen year-complete.** Show every charting for the year with its date, not silently the first, and let a new one be created. This is a data-visibility bug independent of any visit link, and it is what a dentist would notice first.
      2. **Link the charting to the visit:** nullable `preventive_id` on `DENTAL_CHART`. Then a charting BELONGS to Visit 1 or Visit 2 instead of being guessed at by date, and Record Visit can offer *"chart now"*, creating the chart already attached.
      3. **Let the reports read the link.** `useDohReportData`'s 1st/2nd application stops being **inferred from chart dates** — its own comment calls that "an interpretation worth understanding" — and the TCL's per-visit tooth counts become fact, the same move Sprint 147 made for the service ticks.
    - **⚠ `TREATMENT` is NOT the right home for this and should stay where it is.** The user's phrase is *charting to treatment*: what a visit DOES to teeth is `TOOTH_RECORD.treatment_code` on that visit's chart. `TREATMENT` is the free-text chairside note (diagnosis / treatment_done) and answers a different question.
    - **⚠ Existing charts have no visit.** `preventive_id` must be nullable and old rows stay null — the date-based inference remains the fallback for them, exactly as Sprint 147 kept the chart fallback for visits recorded before it.


62. **~~RECORDING AN RPC VISIT IS ALSO RECORDING TREATMENT~~ — BUILT 2026-09-05 as Sprint 147.** Original note:  **⚠ user, 2026-09-05. `PREVENTIVE_CARE_RECORD` STORES NO SERVICES AT ALL.** NOT scoped; it is a model change, so it needs approval.
    - **The model has four fields:** `iptr_id`, `visit_date`, `visit_number`, `facility_based`. That is it. **A visit records that someone was seen, never what was done to them.**
    - **The Record Visit modal saves exactly those** (`RPCTracking.tsx:78` → `POST /preventive-care-records` with date, number, facility flag). The dentist ticks nothing.
    - **But CLAUDE.md's own module 5 says an RPC visit IS a set of services:** *"oral screening, prophylaxis, fluoride varnish, hygiene instruction, caries risk assessment"* — and page 2 of the Target Client List prints exactly those as per-visit tick columns for FIRST and SECOND visit.
    - **⚠ THIS IS WHY SEVERAL FILED FIGURES ARE APPROXIMATIONS, and the code already says so in three places.** `useRPCTracking`'s own comment: *"this is 'the student has had this treatment', NOT 'this was done at the RPC visit' — PREVENTIVE_CARE_RECORD stores no services at all, so per-visit treatment data does not exist to filter on."* The TCL therefore answers "did this pupil ever have FV?" where the form asks "was FV done at this visit?", and `useDohReportData` derives 1st/2nd application from CHART DATES because nothing records the ordinal.
    - **RECOMMENDED SHAPE — service ticks ON the visit, not a join:** add to `PREVENTIVE_CARE_RECORD` the columns the form actually prints — `oral_screening`, `oral_prophylaxis`, `fluoride_varnish`, `oral_hygiene_instruction`, and the visit's `caries_risk` (Low/Moderate/High). Booleans default **false**, risk defaults **null**. The Record Visit modal grows a tick row. **The TCL's per-visit columns then read the visit instead of guessing from the chart, and 1st/2nd application stops being inferred from dates.**
    - **⚠ Do NOT route this through TREATMENT or TOOTH_RECORD.** `TREATMENT` is free-text diagnosis/treatment_done for a chairside entry; `TOOTH_RECORD` is per-tooth, and a fluoride varnish or a prophylaxis is whole-mouth — there is no tooth to hang it on. Forcing either would invent per-tooth data the dentist never recorded.
    - **⚠ ERD deviation** — same treatment as `REFERRAL` got: build it, then update `docs/DATA-MODEL.md` and the Chapter 3 figure.
    - **⚠ Existing rows have no answer.** Every visit recorded before this change must read as *not recorded*, never as "not done" — the same rule `facility_based` already follows (default null, and FHSIS prints "not recorded" rather than inventing the split). Booleans defaulting false would silently claim no service was given.
    - **Distinct from #55**, which is about structured entry on the CHART. This is about the visit.


61. **~~THERE ARE TWO IPTR VERSIONS AND BOTH ARE VALID~~ — BUILT 2026-09-05 as Sprint 137.** Both forms are offered; the PDF button is now a choice. ⚠ The photo of Form 1 is NOT in the repo (real patient data - see that sprint). Original note:
    - The user has now confirmed what backlog **#37** suspected: the Taguig City Health Office **"INDIVIDUAL PATIENT TREATMENT RECORD"** (manuscript Appendix G, the one Sprint 135 built) and the **"Form 3 / INDIVIDUAL TREATMENT RECORD"** they photographed on 2026-09-03 are **both in use**. Neither supersedes the other.
    - **So the app must OFFER BOTH, not choose.** Sprint 135's `IptrForm` is version A; version B needs its own component built to its own scan, and the PDF button becomes a choice ("which form?") rather than a single action.
    - **Before building version B:** get a legible scan of it into the repo the way Appendix G already is. The 2026-09-03 photo was a phone snap; the Appendix G images are embedded base64 in the manuscript and are what made Sprint 135 possible.
    - ⚠ **Do not "reconcile" the two into one merged form.** Two valid forms filed with the same office are two documents; merging them would produce a third that is neither, which is exactly what the form-fidelity rule forbids.


60. **⚠ THE "IPTR PDF" IS A SCREENSHOT OF THE APP, NOT THE DOH FORM — found 2026-09-05, SCOPED, not built.** `DentalChart.tsx:875` captures `recordRef`, which spans lines 953-1832: the patient-info card, the tab strip, the Edit buttons, whatever tab happens to be open. **That is the document a family or a referral receives.** Under CLAUDE.md's form rule ("reproduce every page … the form is the form") and the printout rule, it is the wrong artifact.
    - **Both official pages are ON THIS MACHINE and are LEGIBLE** (unlike the TCL scans): manuscript **Appendix G**, `image19` = page 1, `image20` = page 2, 576x740 each.
    - **PAGE 1, read from the scan:** DOH / City of Taguig header · **Personal Information** (Patient's Name, Birthday, Age, Sex, Address, Occupation, Contact #, PhilHealth Principal/Dependent, 4Ps/NHTS) · a **Year 1 … Year 5 matrix** whose rows are DATE EXAMINED, then **Medical History** (Allergies, Hypertension/CVA, Diabetes Mellitus, Blood Disorders, Cardiovascular/Heart, Thyroid, Hepatitis, Malignancy, History of Previous Hospitalization, Medical (Last Admission & Cause), Surgical (Post-Operative), Blood transfusion (Month & Year), Tattoo, Others), **Dietary Habits and Social History** (Sugar Sweetened Beverages, Alcohol Drinker, Tobacco User, Betel Nut Chewer, Body Piercing, Nail Biting, Thumbsucking) and **Oral Health Condition** (Orally Fit, Dental Caries, Gingivitis, Periodontal Diseases, Debris, Calculus, Abnormal Growth, Cleft Lip/Palate, Completely Edentulous, Others) · the **Filipino consent paragraph** · **Lagda ng Pasyente / Lagda ng Magulang o Guardian** · and a visit table: **Date · Weight · Temp · BP · Chief Complaint · Diagnosis · Treatment Done · Dentist · Signature · Remarks**.
    - **PAGE 2:** **Dental Charting** — FIVE tooth charts, one per year, each with its own Date, the FDI grid with Treatment and Condition rows and a d/m/f/x + D/M/F/X/T summary — plus **Legend: Condition** (√ Sound/Sealed · D/d Decayed · M/m Missing · F/f Filled · DX/dx Indicated for Extraction · Un/un Unerupted · S/s Supernumerary · JC/jc Jacket Crown · P/p Pontic) and **Legend: Treatment** (FV Fluoride Varnish · PFS Pit and Fissure Sealant · PF Permanent Filling · TF Temporary Filling · X Extraction · SDF Silver Diamine Fluoride), ending in **Signature of Examining Dentist**.
    - **✅ The data model fits the form almost exactly, which is why this is worth doing:** the Year 1-5 matrix IS five `STUDENT_IPTR` years; its three row-blocks are `MEDICAL_HISTORY`, `DIETARY_SOCIAL_HABITS` and `ORAL_HEALTH_CONDITION` field for field; page 2's five charts are `DENTAL_CHART` + `TOOTH_RECORD` per year; the legends confirm the codes the app already uses.
    - **What has NO source and must print BLANK** (never omitted): Occupation (no model stores it — the OCR note says the same), and the visit table's **Temp**, **BP**, **Chief Complaint** and **Signature** columns. Weight exists (`STUDENT_IPTR.weight_kg`, Sprint 68); Date/Diagnosis/Treatment Done/Dentist/Remarks come from `TREATMENT`.
    - **Scope when approved:** a real `IptrForm` component built to the scan — two printable pages with `.form-print` — replacing the `recordRef` screen capture as the PDF source. It is the biggest single form left, and it is the one a parent actually holds.


59. **Bulk upload of student records from Excel — ⚠ ALREADY BUILT (Sprint 23m). The request is really about the FORMAT.** User 2026-09-05: *"add a button bulk upload student records in students tab using excel, but i dont know the excel format yet or have excel files yet… this is also needed for data cleaning right? for prediction right"*.
    - **It exists and takes .xlsx.** `PatientList.tsx` has a bulk-import modal (`showBulkUpload`, `buildBulkRow`, `bulkStep`) that reads **CSV or .xlsx** (ExcelJS, `wb.xlsx.load`), previews the rows, and reports parse errors. **Do not build a second one** — look at this first.
    - **The format it expects today:** required **Last Name, First Name, Sex, Grade Level, Section, Birthday, Address**; optional **Middle Name, Contact Number**. Sprint 120/121's value rules apply on both sides (birthday bounds 3-25, names capped 60, PH phone structure).
    - **So the real open question is whose format wins** when the school's actual roster file arrives: bend the importer to the school's columns, or hand the school a template. **Cannot be answered until a real file exists** — that is the blocker the user named, not the button.
    - **On *"can upload IPTR image also be bulk?"* — NO today, and the reason is not the file input.** `PatientList.tsx:896` takes ONE file (`e.target.files?.[0]`, no `multiple`), image or PDF, and OCR runs **in the browser** via Tesseract.
      - **Adding `multiple` is trivial; making it USABLE is not.** Every scan produces findings a human must review before anything is saved — Sprints 86/87 made that a rule, and the reader declines rather than guesses (an upside-down page once reported 31 phantom findings on a blank form). A 40-scan batch therefore produces 40 review screens, not 40 saved pupils, so the sprint is really a REVIEW QUEUE: run them in sequence, hold the results, let the aide accept/correct/skip each, then commit.
      - ⚠ **Browser-side Tesseract is the practical limit.** One page already takes seconds; a class set run in one tab will lock the UI unless the work is queued and progressed one file at a time. Measure with a real batch before promising it.
      - **Order to build in, if it is wanted:** the review queue first (it is the actual work), `multiple` on the input last (it is one attribute).
    - **On *"needed for data cleaning / for prediction"* — RELATED, BUT NOT THE SAME PATH, and it matters that these do not get conflated:**
      - The ML pipeline does NOT read the app. It reads `data/raw` offline: `clean_excel.py` → `build_features.py` → `run_experiments.py`. Nothing there depends on this button.
      - Where they DO meet: the 50 hand-encoded records (decided 2026-09-01). If the dentist encodes them in Excel, ONE spreadsheet layout could feed both the importer and `clean_excel.py` — which is an argument for settling the format once, for both, rather than twice.
      - ⚠ **The importer only covers STUDENT identity fields.** The ML features are DMF/dmf, oral conditions, dietary habits, medical history — none of which this importer accepts. **Bulk-uploading rosters does not produce training data**, and treating it as though it does would be the mistake to avoid here.


58. **~~Day-note dialog: split it into two halves~~ — BUILT 2026-09-05 as Sprint 131** (see that section; two items left unverified there). Original scoping: Their words: *"how about make it two halves one part is to fill in add note, the other half is the showing of current sched and notes instead of topping each other"*.
    - **The cause is one line, and it is not the layout — it is the WIDTH.** The day panel is a `Modal` opened with `maxWidth="max-w-md"` (`Appointments.tsx:683`), i.e. ~448px. At that width a two-column split is unusable, so everything stacks: **Appointments** (the day's schedule) → **Notes** (existing notes) → the add-note textarea and button. On a busy day the write box is pushed below the fold, which is exactly the "topping each other" complaint.
    - **How I would implement it:** widen the modal to `max-w-3xl`, then inside it `grid grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2 gap-4` — LEFT = schedule + existing notes (the read half), RIGHT = the add-note form (the write half, always visible). Give the left column `max-h-[60vh] overflow-y-auto` so a long day scrolls inside its own half instead of pushing the form away.
    - **On *"same function as the box below the Today/Upcoming/Completed/Missed/Rotation buttons"* — the answer is REUSE, not rebuild.** That box renders `<AppointmentCard a={a} showActions />` (`Appointments.tsx:372`, used at 573/597/615/630). With `showActions` it already gives: **Mark Completed** and **Mark Missed** on a Scheduled session, **Mark Completed** on one In Progress, and **re-open to Scheduled** on a Completed/Missed one — plus a `Link to="/dental-charts"` titled "Open Dental Charts". The day dialog today reimplements none of that: it has only a per-appointment note editor.
      - `AppointmentCard` is declared INSIDE the `Appointments` component, so the day dialog can use it as-is with no extraction and no prop plumbing.
      - **⚠ THE ONE REAL MISMATCH TO SOLVE FIRST: the two lists are at different granularities.** `AppointmentCard` takes an `AppointmentSession` (one card per time+grade+section), while the day dialog lists **individual students inside** each session (`st.appointmentId`, `st.name`, `st.notes`). Dropping the card in wholesale would lose the per-student rows. Decide deliberately: either the read half shows session cards WITH the student rows nested underneath, or the per-student actions get lifted into the card. **This is the design decision of the sprint — everything else is layout.**
      - ⚠ The card's chart link goes to the LIST (`/dental-charts`), not to that pupil's chart. If "open chart" is meant to land on the pupil, that is a small separate improvement — the student rows in the day dialog know the id, the card does not.
    - **⚠ Keep the stack below `md:`.** CLAUDE.md's three device classes apply: at ~390px two columns are worse than one, so the phone keeps today's single column with the form last. This is a widescreen improvement, not a layout replacement.
    - **On *"maybe can remove or delete?"* — half of it already exists, half does not, and neither is a delete.**
      - **DAY NOTES already have it:** each note in that list carries an X (`archiveDayNote`, Sprint 108), and Sprint 108 had to widen `archiveRoles` so the dentist and aide could actually use it. Nothing to build; the split just has to keep that control reachable in the read half.
      - **APPOINTMENTS in this panel do NOT.** The only per-appointment control there is edit-notes (`setApptNoteId` → textarea → Save/Cancel). Cancelling or removing an appointment has to be done elsewhere. **That is the actual gap**, and it is worth confirming with the user that appointments — not just notes — are what they want removable from this dialog.
      - **On *"can edit also"* — this one genuinely does not exist, and the API is already there.** `Appointments.tsx` calls exactly two day-note endpoints: `POST /day-notes` (280) and `PATCH /day-notes/:id/archive` (322). **There is no edit path in the UI at all**, so correcting a typo today means archiving the note and retyping it — which leaves the wrong note in the archive forever. The route's `PUT /:id` already accepts `CLINICAL_WRITE_ROLES`, so this is UI-only: inline edit on the note row (click to open a textarea, Save / Cancel), `apiClient.put`, exactly the shape the per-appointment note editor beside it already uses.
      - ⚠ **It can never be a DELETE.** CLAUDE.md: never hard delete any record. Both cases are ARCHIVE (`PATCH /:id/archive`), recoverable by System Admin from `/archive`. Label the control "Remove" in the UI if that reads better, but say archive in the code and keep restore admin-only.
    - **Check while in there:** the same dialog is where a note is archived (Sprint 108's remove button), so the read half must keep that control reachable after the split.


57. **Printouts must contain ONLY the form — user rule 2026-09-05, NOT scoped.** Their words: *"the print out should just be the forms do not include headers or somethings from the webpage not inside the forms"*. Now also a standing rule in CLAUDE.md.
    - **What the print CSS does today** (`src/styles/index.css:76-140`): hides `aside`, `header`, `nav`, `button`, `[role="navigation"]` and `.doh-report-controls`, sets landscape, and zooms `#doh-report-printable` to 0.45.
    - **⚠ What it does NOT hide, and therefore still prints** (read from the CSS and the DOM, not yet confirmed on paper): the page title block ("Reports · DOH Consolidated Report & Internal Reports"), the `<label>` text beside the hidden `<select>`s ("School:", "School year:"), and every explanatory caption — "Every column of the paper form is shown…", "Covering school year …", "Sep 2026 — showing 0 clients consulted…". Those explain the APP; none of them is on the DOH form.
    - **The fix is a different STRATEGY, not more selectors.** Hiding chrome element by element is why this keeps leaking as the UI grows. Print by inclusion: give each report ONE printable root, hide everything outside it in `@media print`, and move anything that genuinely belongs on paper inside it.
    - **Check the PDF/Excel exports the same way** — `exportDohReportToPdf` captures a ref, so whatever sits inside that element goes into the PDF too; the captions may already be in the filed PDF.
    - **PARTLY VERIFIED 2026-09-05 without opening the print dialog.** `window.print()` freezes the automated browser, so instead the SHIPPED `@media print` rules were copied onto the screen and screenshotted — 1 print block, 15 style rules, i.e. the real stylesheet, not a re-typed copy of it.
      - **DOH Consolidated: PASSES.** Only the table, its DOH title row and the "Prepared by" footer. No sidebar, no page heading, no tab strip, no `School:` / `School year:` labels, none of the explanatory captions.
      - **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (Sprint 133): the footnotes are DROPPED from paper.** User chose the form-fidelity rule over the note-why rule. New `.print-hide` class, applied to the School Summary's five explanatory notes, hides them in `@media print` while they stay on screen beside the table they describe. **Re-verified the same way: the printed sheet is now the table alone**, and the notes render normally again once the emulation is removed. Use `print-hide` for any future on-screen explanation that lives inside a printable root - do not move such notes out of the root, because on screen they belong with their table.
      - **School Summary: passed for chrome, and raised the QUESTION now resolved above.** The sheet prints alone — and so do its five explanatory footnotes ("MALE / FEMALE count students; each TOTAL counts teeth", "Very Good (VG) is left blank…", "Rows follow the printed sheet exactly…"). **Those are not on the paper form.** They sit inside the `.form-print` root, so inclusion-printing keeps them. Two rules pull opposite ways: *a printout is the form and nothing else* says drop them; *leave a cell blank and note why* says keep them. **ASK THE USER — do not decide this silently.** If dropped, the mechanism is a `print-hide` class on those blocks, not another root.
      - **STILL UNVERIFIED, and only a human can do it:** pagination, `@page landscape`, the `zoom: 0.45` on the wide table, and whether `position: absolute` leaves a blank first page. Emulating the rules on screen cannot show page breaks.
    - **Verify on actual output, not by reading CSS:** print-preview each of the seven report tabs, or export the PDF and open it. This item is only closed when a printed sheet is indistinguishable from the official form.


56. **The real Target Client List is TWO PAGES; ours is one — ⚠ ANSWERED 2026-09-05, and it is NOT a missing-column problem. Both pages were read.**

Extract with a few lines of base64 and read them; no other laptop and no workbook are needed. *(The earlier note saying the workbook is on the other laptop was true of the .xlsx and irrelevant — the form itself was in the repo all along.)*

Under CLAUDE.md's form rule ("reproduce every page … the form is the form"), the output should split the same way: **page 1 = identity + oral health status; page 2 = the visit and services groups, repeating `No.`**.

CLAUDE.md's form rule was strengthened the same day to say so, because the earlier wording ("keep all rows and columns") let a whole missing PAGE pass. **The open question below is only HOW, never whether.**

These need different work — (a) is a data gap, (b) is a print stylesheet job, (c) is a second report. **Do not start until the page-2 heading row has been read — read the whole workbook end to end this time, not the part already transcribed.**

55. **Structured TREATMENT entry: dropdowns for treatment and condition, plus tooth numbers on both — user idea 2026-09-05, NOT scoped, to be done after the current workflow.** Their words: *"noting or adding or what treatment can be dropdown, adding condition can be drop down and can also indicated teeth numbers on them both, i dont know how to implement any thoughts?"*
    - **The vocabulary already exists and must not be reinvented.** `DentalChart.tsx` exports `treatmentCodes` (OEX, OP, FV, PFS, TR, TF, X, SDF …) and holds the condition codes; the DOH reports, the TCL and `useDohReportData` all read those exact codes. A second, parallel list typed into a Treatment form would drift from them within a sprint and the forms would disagree.
    - **Tooth numbers also already exist**, as `TOOTH_RECORD` rows keyed by FDI number under a `DENTAL_CHART`. So the honest question is not "where do we store teeth" but **which store is the source of truth**.
    - **⚠ THE REAL DESIGN RISK: two places would then answer "which teeth were treated?"** — the chart and the treatment entry — and they can disagree. Whatever is built, ONE of them must be the source and the other must read it.
    - **My recommendation, to argue with rather than accept:** keep `TOOTH_RECORD` as the source. The Treatment form gains (a) a treatment dropdown of the existing codes, (b) a condition dropdown, (c) a tooth-number picker, and **writes tooth records** for the selected teeth on the selected chart, while `TREATMENT.diagnosis`/`treatment_done` stay as the free-text narrative. Nothing new is stored twice, and the DOH tooth-count columns light up from the same rows they already read.
    - **Why it is worth doing at all, beyond convenience:** the DOH forms count TEETH (`Composite Filling (Tooth Count)`, `Extraction (Tooth Count)`, the SDF applications). Free text cannot be counted; a code plus a tooth list can. It also removes spelling variance from a filed form.
    - **Open questions for the dentist before building:** does she record a treatment per tooth or per visit? Is a condition ever recorded WITHOUT a tooth (soft-tissue findings)? Should picking teeth be a chart click or a number list on phones?

54. **~~A NEW MODEL HAS A CHECKLIST, and nothing enforces it~~ — ENFORCED 2026-09-05 as part of Sprint 129.** `purgeDemoData.ts` now refuses to run unless every registered model is in the plan or explicitly excluded with a reason; proven by removing `Referral` and watching it refuse. The checklist below is still the reference for the OTHER places a new model must be registered — those are not yet guarded. Original note: Their words: *"things or variables or something we add might be needed to be purge demo something idea"*. **This is not hypothetical: Sprint 127's `Referral` was already missed.** The code review found `purgeDemoData.ts`'s plan lists every IPTR-child collection except the one added that day, so `npm run purge:demo -- --confirm` before deployment would delete the demo students and their IPTRs and **leave every referral behind, orphaned, pointing at `iptr_id`s that no longer exist** — invisible in every report (the join drops them) and permanent, since nothing is hard-deleted afterwards.
    - **The places a new model has to be registered, from tracing `Referral` through the codebase:** `models/index.ts` · a `createCrudRouter` block in `routes/index.ts` · `RULES` in `schoolScope.ts` · **`purgeDemoData.ts`'s plan** · `ArchiveManagement.tsx`'s `KINDS` (or an archived record is invisible and unrestorable) · `docs/DATA-MODEL.md` · the Chapter 3 ERD figure (user-only) · `is_demo` if seeders will create rows of it.
    - **A note is the weak version; a GUARD is the fix.** `purgeDemoData.ts` can assert coverage: enumerate the registered Mongoose models and REFUSE to run when one is neither in the plan nor on an explicit exclusion list with a stated reason. That converts "someone must remember" into "the script stops" — and it is the same principle as the 2026-09-04 lesson, since a purge that silently skips a collection reports success having compared nothing.
    - Scope estimate: the guard is one function inside `purgeDemoData.ts` plus a short exclusion list; the checklist belongs in CLAUDE.md, where it constrains every session, not in HANDOFF.

0. **"Double entry — doctor can choose" — ~~open~~ DONE 2026-09-01 as Sprint 47** (see that section above for the design, the verification, and the one untested path). Raised 2026-08-27 as *"for double entry, doctor can choose they said"*. **User confirmed it means (a): a duplicate STUDENT record** — the same child encoded twice (e.g. once by OCR, once by hand). The other readings (appointment double-booking, conflicting IPTR entries) are NOT what was meant and are not in scope. **Still open if ever wanted: merging duplicates already in the database** — explicitly declined for Sprint 47, and the notes below are kept because that build would need them.
    - **Decided scope = WARN AT ENTRY ONLY.** Merging/deduplicating records already in the DB was offered and declined — no archiving, no repointing of child records, no referential-integrity work. Because nothing is ever archived, the Student `archiveRoles` question is moot for this sprint.
    - **Decided actors = dentist + dental aide + system admin** can all resolve a warning ("save anyway"). No permission change needed: all three are already in `CLINICAL_WRITE_ROLES`/admin for student writes.
    - **Findings from the 2026-09-01 code read (don't re-derive):**
      - Students have **no duplicate guard at all**. `uniqueBy` exists (`crudFactory.ts:42`) but is wired only to `student-iptrs` (`routes/index.ts:112`); Student gets `createCrudRouter(Student, { writeRoles: CLINICAL_WRITE_ROLES })`.
      - **A name-based `uniqueBy` is impossible** — name fields are encrypted with random IVs (Sprint 26), so server-side equality never matches. Workable rule: plaintext indexed prefilter on `birthday + school_id` (both unencrypted), then decrypt + compare normalized `last_name`+`first_name` on that handful. Ignore `middle_name` (OCR often drops it); show `sex`/grade in the dialog but keep them out of the match key.
      - **Three creation paths**, so the check belongs at the API, not on one button: add form (`PatientList.tsx:311`), bulk CSV/xlsx import (`:208`), and OCR.
      - `ApiError` carries only `status` + `message` (`src/app/api/client.ts:4-10`) — needs a body/details field to pass candidates to the dialog.
      - `queueProcessor.ts:93` maps any non-401/403 4xx to `markFailed("The server rejected this change (error N)")` and `:121` treats that as queue-blocking. Blocking is correct per the FIFO rule, but a 409 needs a real message or the aide cannot clear the queue.
    - **Proposed shape:** POST /students returns **409 + candidate list** when a likely match exists, unless the body carries `confirm_duplicate: true`; the client dialog offers *Open existing* / *Save anyway*, and the re-POST sets the flag. Guards all three paths and offline replay for free. Bulk import must NOT modal per row — collect matches into the existing failure summary as "skipped as possible duplicates".
0b. **Pagination / unbounded reads — PART 1 DONE as Sprint 48 (2026-09-02); the LIST screens remain open.** The detail-screen half is fixed: `GET /` now accepts whitelisted foreign-key filters and the IPTR screen asks for one student's rows instead of the whole database (−92.6% measured). **What is still open is Option 2** — server-side `?page&limit`, moving filter/sort server-side, and summary endpoints for the counts, covering `useRPCTracking` / `useStudents` / `useDohReportData` / `useRiskClassification`, which are lists over ALL students and so cannot use the Sprint 48 fix. It is now cheaper than when this was written, because the filter mechanism and its ObjectId validation already exist. Original note follows. Report from someone reviewing the build: *"iptr takes too long to load because it retrieves all, advised pagination, retrieve only 10?"* **The diagnosis is right, the prescribed fix is only half of it.** Every list hook fetches whole collections and joins them client-side — `useRPCTracking` alone now pulls SIX (`/students`, `/schools`, `/student-iptrs`, `/preventive-care-records`, `/dental-charts`, `/tooth-records`, the last two added in Sprint 39). At ~8,000 students that's megabytes of JSON, decrypted server-side per record, before anything paints. **Page-size 10 alone does NOT fix it** — the filters, counts, and summary tiles are all computed client-side over the full set, so paginating the fetch breaks them unless the filtering and aggregation move server-side too. Recommended order when scoped: (1) server-side `?page&limit` + `?fields` projection on the list routes, (2) move filter/sort params server-side, (3) return counts/aggregates from a dedicated summary endpoint rather than deriving them from the full list, (4) only then paginate the UI. Page size 25–50 reads better than 10 for a clinic worklist. **Interacts with the offline queue** — cached list pages and optimistic pending writes (`usePendingWritesFor`) assume the full set is present.
0c. ~~**Sync status: icon, not banner**~~ — **DONE as Sprint 40 (2026-08-27).** Original request: *"online offline not banner just icon top right corner rotating arrows if syncing, page do not go blank and refresh but background refresh"*. Three parts: (a) replace the full-width `OfflineBanner` strip with a compact status icon pinned top-right; (b) rotating-arrows spinner while syncing; (c) **refresh in the background — the page must never blank out or hard-reload**. Part (c) is the substantial one and is NOT purely presentational: it means list hooks re-fetch and swap data in place while keeping the current view mounted (no full-page loading skeleton on refresh). Touches `OfflineBanner.tsx`, the top bar, and every list hook's loading state. **Note the Sprint 33 gotcha before starting:** the mobile top bar must NOT be `fixed` — `OfflineBanner` renders in normal flow above `RouterProvider`, and a fixed bar covers it. Moving the indicator INTO the top bar may make that constraint obsolete; re-check it rather than assuming.
0d. **Sign-in autofill — BROWSER-DELEGATED HALF DONE 2026-09-02 (Sprint 50); the localStorage email-prefill half is NOT built and is probably unnecessary now.** Re-raised by a classmate's note (*"Log-in — auto-fill login credentials (remember me) — landing page | sign in page, already done?"*). **Answer: half.** Sprint 37's "Keep me signed in on this device" checkbox controls SESSION PERSISTENCE and works; autofill did not exist — verified in code and matching the live DOM read of 2026-08-28 (`email:none`, `password:none`).
    - **Done:** `autoComplete="username"` + `name="email"` on the email input and `autoComplete="current-password"` + `name="password"` on the password input (`Login.tsx`). The form was already a real `<form onSubmit>` with a `type="submit"` button, which is the other precondition, so browsers now offer to save and fill. **Nothing is stored by the app** — no localStorage, no OWASP exposure.
    - **⚠ The fix forced a second change, and skipping it would have CREATED a bug:** once browsers start saving credentials, they offer them into every unmarked password field. So all five password-SETTING fields got `autoComplete="new-password"` — `ResetPassword.tsx` (new + confirm) and `AccountManagement.tsx` (temporary password on create, plus admin-set new + confirm). Without it the browser would fill the signed-in admin's own password into a field that sets ANOTHER user's.
    - **Still open (small, and arguably now pointless):** prefilling the email from localStorage when "remember me" was ticked. The browser's credential manager does this already; build it only if someone specifically wants it without a saved credential.
    - **"landing page | sign in page" in the note: there is no separate landing page.** `/` IS the sign-in page; the only thing calling itself a landing page is `ResetPassword.tsx`, the target for emailed reset links. A public pre-auth landing page would be new scope, and for an internal-only clinic tool it is not obviously wanted.
    Original note follows. Follow-on from Sprint 37: when the box was ticked, prefill the email on the next visit. **Decide first: email only, or email + password?** Email-only is a one-line localStorage read and safe. Password autofill should be delegated to the BROWSER's own credential manager (correct `autoComplete="username"` / `"current-password"` attributes + a real form submit), never stored by the app — storing a password in localStorage would undo Sprint 37's whole point and is an OWASP finding waiting to happen at defense. Check whether `Login.tsx` inputs even carry `autoComplete` attributes today; adding them may be most of the win for free.
0e. **Camera scan for IPTR — SCOPED + APPROACH DECIDED 2026-08-28, then PARKED by the user before building. STILL PARKED; the LABEL was fixed instead 2026-09-02.** User spotted the mismatch: *"it says scan iptr but it is upload, icon is also scan"* — the button carried a `ScanLine` icon and the verb "Scan", both promising a camera that does not exist. Renamed to **"Upload IPTR Form"** with an `Upload` icon (button, modal title, dropzone icon); the OCR extraction is still described inside the modal, and the `ScanLine` on the "Pre-filled from scanned IPTR form" notice stays because OCR genuinely did scan it. **Rename it back if 0e ever ships** — a comment at the call site says so. Request: *"upload iptr and use camera scan"*. **Upload already exists and works** — `PatientList.tsx` "Upload IPTR Form" modal has drag-drop + browse, accepts JPG/PNG/PDF, calls `handleOcrFile` (`PatientList.tsx:342`) → dynamic-imports `utils/iptrOcr.ts`. So the delta is CAMERA ONLY; do not rebuild upload.
   - **User chose "Both" when asked** (native camera button on phone/tablet, in-app viewfinder on laptop/PC). Scope estimate given: 2 files (`PatientList.tsx` + new `components/CameraCapture.tsx`), no new models, no new deps, no server changes, medium complexity.
   - Phone/tablet path = `<input type="file" accept="image/*" capture="environment">` → hands off to the OS camera app, returns a `File` straight into the existing `handleOcrFile`. ~15 lines, no permission code.
   - Laptop/PC path = `getUserMedia` viewfinder + framing guide + shutter + retake, canvas → `File` → same `handleOcrFile`. This is the expensive half (permission prompts, denied/no-camera fallbacks, stream teardown on unmount).
   - **Branch on coarse-pointer + touch, NOT screen width** — a laptop can be narrow and a tablet can be wide (three-device-classes rule).
   - ⚠ **`getUserMedia` needs HTTPS.** Fine on Vercel, and `localhost` is exempt — but testing from a phone against a LAN dev IP fails silently. Testing gotcha, not a bug.
   - ⚠ **Expect the desktop webcam path to OCR poorly.** `iptrOcr.ts` is label-anchored full-page OCR needing legible small print; a ~720p unlit webcam shot of an A4 form will not deliver it. The phone path is the one that works in the field. Do not read a weak desktop result as a regression.
   - **Left undecided (user did not answer the upload-side question):** treated as leave-upload-as-is. Separate candidates raised and NOT approved — (a) **iPhone HEIC gap is real**: `accept` is PNG/JPEG/PDF and iOS shoots HEIC by default; the `capture` path usually returns JPEG so the camera button dodges it, but picking an existing photo from an iPhone camera roll can fail, and Tesseract can't decode HEIC either so a fix needs a converter dep (against the no-new-packages rule); (b) show a thumbnail + confirm before running the slow OCR; (c) downscale 12MP photos to ~2000px before OCR.
0f. **Action-feedback consistency — (1) + (2) DONE as Sprint 43 (2026-09-01); (3) judged correct as-is, so this item is CLOSED.** Kept for the audit findings below, which are the durable part. User asked what announces "saved / edited / completed / cancelled": it is a **toast**, and the three-tier system already exists from Sprint 23k — **`Toast.tsx` / `useToast()`** for "the action you just took landed or failed" (floats, auto-dismiss 4s success / 6s error, capped at 3 so a burst of saves can't wallpaper the screen, animated exit), **`Notice.tsx`** for section/page-level status (inline, persistent, mostly form submit errors), and bare red text for field-level validation. Separately `SyncStatus.tsx` owns connection state (Sprint 40) and `UpdateToast.tsx` owns the new-version prompt. Existing coverage: **17 toast call sites across 5 components** (`AccountManagement`, `DentalChart`, `Appointments`, `PatientList`, `Root`), wording already consistent — past tense, terse, full stop.
   - **CONFIRMED gap (read the code): `AIAnalytics.tsx` never imports `useToast`.** It hand-rolls feedback at `AIAnalytics.tsx:559-561` — a green/red `<p>` driven by `saveMessage`, whose variant is chosen by `saveMessage.startsWith('Failed')`. Two problems: the message has no auto-dismiss in that code path, and the success/error styling silently inverts if anyone rewords the error string.
   - **Why this one matters more than a normal inconsistency:** the action is **dentist validation of a risk assessment** — per CLAUDE.md the gate on clinical use, and the most likely thing a panelist asks to see demoed. It is the only mutation in the app whose confirmation looks different from every other.
   - **FULL AUDIT DONE 2026-09-01 — all 34 `apiClient.post/put/patch/delete` call sites walked. Answer to "do we have a toast for every activity?": NO, three paths opt out.** This SUPERSEDES the crude line-count guess below.
   - **(1) THE BIG ONE — the main dental chart save has no toast** (`DentalChart.tsx:404-455`). It is the largest write in the app: tooth records + medical history + dietary/social habits + oral health conditions in one `Promise.all`. Feedback is `setSaved(true)` flipping the **Save button's own label to "Saved!" for 2s** (`DentalChart.tsx:867`), with failures as small red text under the tab row (`:874`). **Why this is worse than it sounds:** the confirmation lives INSIDE the button just clicked — under the user's thumb on a phone — and that button sits at the top of a long scrolling form, so a dentist who edits teeth further down and saves may see no confirmation at all. Directly contradicts Sprint 40's premise that field staff need glanceable state. **Fix first.**
   - **(2) `AIAnalytics.tsx` dentist-validation save** — hand-rolled green/red `<p>` at `:559-561`, variant chosen by `saveMessage.startsWith('Failed')` (inverts silently if the error string is ever reworded), no auto-dismiss in that path. Never imports `useToast`. Clinically the gated action per CLAUDE.md.
   - **(3) `AccountManagement.tsx` 2FA + send-reset-link** (`:56, :70, :86, :220`) — inline `twofaMessage` / `resetError`. **JUDGED CORRECT AS-IS, do not "fix":** 2FA enrolment is a multi-step modal flow where the message must persist beside the code field, not float away after 4s. Same reasoning for `Login.tsx` / `ResetPassword.tsx` using `Notice` — form-level errors belong in the form.
   - ~~**UNVERIFIED, do not treat as fact:** a crude `await api…`-vs-toast line count flagged `AccountManagement.tsx` as 8 saves / 4 toasts.~~ **FALSE POSITIVE, retracted 2026-09-01** — the file was walked and its uncovered paths are the deliberate 2FA/reset ones above. The same crude proxy MISSED the chart save, which is the actual problem. Lesson: count real mutation call sites, not `await api…` lines.
   - **Fix shape when approved (scoped 2026-09-01, 2 files, no new components — the toast already exists):** (a) `DentalChart.tsx` — `toast.success` on the chart save, keep the "Saved!" button label as the in-place echo, move the failure off the cramped `:874` red line onto `toast.error`; (b) `AIAnalytics.tsx` — import `useToast`, delete `saveMessage` state + its `startsWith` branch. Leave AccountManagement/Login/ResetPassword alone per (3).

3. **Sprint 21a-d re-run on real data — REFRAMED 2026-08-06, was mis-stated as "blocked on locating files".** There are no files: Barangay Tanyag's dental records are **manual paper forms**, so real data exists only once hand-encoded. **Sample size DECIDED 2026-09-01: n = 50.** The analysis below already assumed n≈50, so it stands as written. Two things settled with the number:
    - **Stratify on RISK LEVEL ONLY** — the earlier "risk level, school, and grade" is not achievable at 50 (3 risk × 3 schools = 9 cells of ~5 before grade is considered; 11 grades empties most of them). Risk level is the label K-Fold depends on, so it is the stratum; school and grade become descriptive characteristics of the sample. **CLAUDE.md was updated to match** — the selection method has to be recorded in Chapter 3.
    - **Expect wide fold-to-fold spread and do not treat it as a bug.** k=5 over 50 records puts ~10 records in each test fold (roughly 3/4/3 across High/Medium/Low), so a single misclassification moves per-class F1 visibly. This is why per-class support counts are mandatory and why no "best algorithm" claim is made.
    - Chapter 5's Limitations section (written 2026-09-01) already covers this framing and needs no edit — only the `[PENDING]` count itself becomes "50".
    - Not blocking, but noted: if the dentist can reach 75–100 without delaying the schedule, fold sizes double and the metrics get materially more stable. 50 is the defensible floor, not the target.
    Consequences, discussed but NOT yet scoped into a sprint (user asked to note only):
   - **n≈50 = pilot, not a validated model.** K-Fold k=5 → ~10 per fold, 2–4 per class; F1 swings several points on a single misclassification. Report all 5 algorithms with per-class support counts, treat differences as indicative, and put sample size in Ch5 limitations. The synthetic 21a–g run stays in as the pipeline demonstration.
   - **Cap features at ~5** (≈10 records per feature), built around DMF/dmf as primary. More than that and RF/XGBoost memorize the training set and post beautiful meaningless scores. Fewer features also makes the Ch4 decision-tree figure readable.
   - **Dentist labels all 50 by hand** — feasible at this N and a stronger, more defensible labeling basis than a DMF threshold rule. Reduces (does not remove) the need for the DOH risk-classification lecture as a citation.
   - **Stratify** across risk level, the three schools, and grade range; write the selection method down while encoding — "how did you pick these 50?" is the first panel question at this sample size.
   - **OCR can carry the demographic half.** Tesseract.js already extracts name/birthday/age/sex/address/contact/grade/section from paper IPTR forms; dental fields (DMF, conditions, treatments) were never scoped for OCR and stay manual. Test on ~5 forms before assuming a time saving.
   - **Training-data approach NOT settled (2026-08-08).** Under discussion: generate ~8,000 synthetic records calibrated to the real tallies / DOH reports, with the hand-encoded real records (count undecided) held out as a validation set. If synthetic data is used it must be **labelled synthetic** in Ch3 methodology, Ch4, and the figure captions, with sample size and synthetic training stated in the (not-yet-written) Ch5 Limitations. Two consequences to handle before any Ch4 numbers get filled in: CLAUDE.md's instruction to state "real IPTR records were the training data" is false under this design, and rule-generated data produces implausibly high F1 (the model recovers the generation logic), which needs owning in the text rather than being explained at the podium. Use synthetic identities (`Student_001…`), never the real name list — the pipeline drops names anyway, so real names add risk and no signal.
   - **Next concrete step when picked up:** write the encoding spec FIRST (exact column list, the CSV shape `clean_excel.py` expects, stratification targets) — hand-encoding is the expensive part, and a shifting column set means encoding twice.

Safe build order: (1) re-add `.dark` token block (recover via `git show 36902173~1:dental-4-12-main/project/src/styles/theme.css`, retune to brand) + hidden localStorage toggle; (2) migrate surfaces component-by-component (Root → Dashboard → lists → DentalChart → Reports → forms → Login); (3) status/brand colors get explicit `dark:` variants; (4) edge cases — **PDF export + print MUST force LIGHT** (html2canvas captures live DOM), charts onto `--chart-N` tokens, Skeleton/gradeColors/schoolColors; (5) only then expose the toggle. Light stays default (daytime clinic tool).
6. **Load-time work — MEASURED 2026-08-25, then PARKED by the user.** Numbers from a real `vite build`, so nothing here needs re-deriving:
    - `dist/assets/index-*.js` = **980 kB (268 kB gzip)** and holds all 14 page components, because `src/app/routes.tsx` static-imports every one — `/login` parses Reports/AuditTrail/AIAnalytics before the login box paints. Heavy libs ARE already split and lazy: exceljs 940 kB, iptrOcr 495 kB, jspdf 391 kB, html2canvas-pro 246 kB, html2canvas 202 kB, pdf.worker 1255 kB (`.mjs`, so `globPatterns` never precaches it).
    - **⚠ The trap that makes React.lazy alone pointless here:** `vite.config.ts` precaches `**/*.{js,css,html,ico,png,svg,woff2}`, so the SW downloads EVERY chunk on install. Splitting routes into 14 lazy chunks just precaches 14 more. Lazy routes only pay off if the precache is scoped to the shell in the same change.
    - ~~**Real defect worth doing whenever this is unparked:** precache still includes jspdf + html2canvas + html2canvas-pro (~840 kB) even though they are dynamic-import-only.~~ **FIXED 2026-09-02.** `globIgnores` now covers `jspdf*` and `html2canvas*` as well. **Precache went 19 entries / 2117 KiB → 16 entries / 1298 KiB — 819 KiB less downloaded on every device at service-worker install.** Verified under a real SW (`npx vite preview` + `verify_live_smoke.mjs`, 11/11, DOH PDF still downloads 1696 KB).
      - **Trade-off, stated plainly**: those chunks are now fetched from the network on demand and are NOT service-worker cached — `src/sw.ts` has no runtime-caching route for JS, only navigation, Google Fonts and `/api` GETs. So **PDF export needs a connection the first time on each device**; the browser's own HTTP cache may serve it afterwards, but the SW will not. This is exactly how Excel export and OCR already behaved, so it is consistent rather than a new class of limitation.
      - ⚠ Only add a `globIgnores` pattern for a chunk reached exclusively through `import()`. Excluding a statically-imported chunk breaks the app offline.
    - **Route splitting is still NOT done** and still carries the trap below — lazy routes only pay off if the precache is scoped to the shell in the same change.
    - **Why parked:** internal tool, 5 staff, known devices, and the PWA precache makes only the FIRST visit per device pay the download. Also unconfirmed that anyone has actually experienced slow loading — and if the felt slowness is AFTER login, it is API/query time and none of the above touches it.
    - Minor: `html2canvas` AND `html2canvas-pro` both ship (448 kB combined) — pro is ours for oklch support, plain comes via jspdf. Only loads on PDF export.

9. ~~**Optional: enforce the session-start `git pull` with a SessionStart hook**~~ **DONE 2026-09-03 — the item's own condition was met: the stale-HANDOFF collision bit.** A session read the on-disk HANDOFF without pulling while `origin/main` was **43 commits ahead** (Sprints 56–80, built on the other device), summarised the project from it, and gave a confidently wrong answer about the demo seed passwords — stating no rotation script existed when Sprint 75 had built one, rotated all five logins and pushed a HANDOFF warning saying exactly that.
    - **Built in committed `.claude/settings.json`** (not `settings.local.json`, which is per-device and would have protected only the machine that was already wrong). `SessionStart` runs `git fetch -q origin main`, counts `HEAD..origin/main`, and when non-zero emits BOTH a user-visible `systemMessage` and `additionalContext` into the model's context. Silent with `exit 0` when current, so it costs nothing on a normal session. Commit `7e8b301e`.
    - **Fetch only, never auto-pull** — a silent merge mid-session could rewrite files under an in-flight edit or hit a conflict with nobody watching. It reports; the user decides.
    - **CLAUDE.md's sprint loop was rewritten the same day** (`eda76de6`): *"PULL THEN READ, never read then pull"*, with the user's own fallback — `git fetch` + `git rev-list --left-right --count HEAD...origin/main` and state the behind-count — for when a pull is not yet appropriate. The durable rule: **an unpulled tracked file is UNKNOWN, not state**; never claim "nothing is in progress", "X doesn't exist", or "the last sprint was N" from one.
    - ⚠ A `SessionStart` hook cannot fire in the session that creates it, and the settings watcher only watches directories that had a settings file at startup. It should load on the next launch on either machine; if it does not, open `/hooks` once to reload config.

Notes worth keeping: SVM has no substantive backing in the current Ch2 (one passing mention in Kang), so the paragraph defends it as a *margin-based comparison baseline*, not an expected winner; and "K-Fold" appears nowhere in the manuscript despite Ch3 selecting the final model by K-Fold F1. 16. **PARKED 2026-08-08 — template/zonal OCR.** Idea raised: supply a blank IPTR template so only the filled-in values get extracted. Findings from looking at the current code, kept so this isn't re-derived: `iptrOcr.ts` does **full-page OCR + label-anchored regex** (`:56`) — Tesseract reads everything including printed template text and has no blank-vs-filled concept, so the printed labels ARE the template knowledge today. Zonal OCR (Tesseract.js `rectangle` per field) is buildable but needs every upload registered/deskewed to the template first, or the boxes read the wrong text — phone photos at an angle break it, and proper registration is OpenCV-class work outside current scope. **The real ceiling is handwriting**, not zoning: Tesseract is trained on printed text and IPTR forms are filled by hand, so zonal OCR would improve *where* it looks, not whether the handwriting is legible. Defensible position stays "OCR assists data entry, operator reviews every field" — which the existing confidence-tint UI already implies. Cheapest next step if ever revisited: run the CURRENT pipeline against a few real filled forms before building anything; that also settles the still-unverified assumption that OCR halves manual encoding time.

Findings so far: **passwords are already safe** — `seedDemo.ts:58-62` `requireEnv()` reads `SEED_DENTIST_PASSWORD` etc. from `.env` and throws rather than falling back to a hardcoded default (Sprint 15.5 fix), and `Login.tsx` shows no demo credentials, only `your.email@floral.com` placeholders. So the exposure is DB-side accounts (`dentist@floral.com`, `aide@…`, plus `seedAdmin.ts`), not anything shipped in the bundle. **DECIDED 2026-08-11: the purge runs from Claude Code as a maintenance script, NOT as an in-app feature.** That was the user's call when asked, and it matters — an in-app "delete demo data" button would have needed an explicit carve-out from the `NEVER hard delete` rule, whereas admin tooling that never ships to a clinic user does not. Write it as `server/scripts/purgeDemoData.ts` alongside the seeders, targeting exactly what the seeders create (demo users by email, the 18 `STUDENTS` names, and their IPTR/chart/preventive/risk/RPC children) so it can never touch a real record. **Also required by the user: no fabricated or placeholder values anywhere in the UI — sample data is acceptable only until it is purged, and every figure must be computed from the DB.** The Sprint A clinic summary already satisfies this (every cell reads live state; footer and overdue clause suppress themselves when empty). **SCRIPT WRITTEN 2026-08-11, NEVER RUN — not even a dry run.** `npm run purge:demo` (`server/scripts/purgeDemoData.ts`), typechecks clean, dry-run by default, `--confirm` to act. **It is UNTESTED against the database; dry-run it and read the output before trusting a single line of it.** Design: students matched against the exact 18 names `seedStudents.ts` creates, so a hand-encoded student is untouchable even if it is run by accident against real data; everything else deleted by foreign key from those students, never a blanket query; `SEED_ADMIN_EMAIL` is explicitly protected and the script refuses to start if that variable is unset — **deleting it would lock the operator out of the deployed app**; it prints "Admin account intact: YES/NO" at the end. It DOES clear `AuditTrail` and `DentistRotation` wholesale, which is the one part not scoped by foreign key — check that is wanted before confirming. Reseed order afterwards: `seed:demo` → `seed:students` → `seed:rpc-visit2` → `seed:iptr-details`.

Note `seedStudents.ts` **cannot be imported directly** — it calls `main()` at module scope, so importing it would RUN the seeder; that is why the array had to move to its own module.

Correctly left alone; they are invisible in the app. They WOULD become orphans of the deleted schools, which is harmless while archived — **do not add them to the purge list without thinking, and never hard-delete them outside this sanctioned dev tooling.**

All six scripts now import it. 19. ~~DMFT History duplicate school year~~ **FIXED 2026-08-11, same day it was spotted** (`ceac46a1` + `fbc14d0f`). Exactly one such pair existed in the whole DB (22 IPTRs, 20 students). Three-part fix: **(1)** `npm run fix:duplicate-iptr` — deletes only records that are BOTH duplicates AND provably empty across all seven child models, never every copy of a group, dry-run by default. Hard delete is deliberate: archiving leaves the row and a restore would resurrect the duplicate; this is dev-machine tooling, never an app feature, which is the line CLAUDE.md draws. Ran against `floral` — deleted 1, kept the charted one, re-run reports 0. **(2)** `crudFactory` gained `uniqueBy`; `student-iptrs` declares `["student_id", "school_year"]` so a duplicate POST 409s from any client. Archived rows count toward the check. **(3)** `handleAddYear` guards on in-flight state and both buttons disable. `fig-4.1.3c` recaptured: two years, "Years tracked: 2".

21. **Audit finding — "user login trail visible in browser DevTools" (reported 2026-08-25, NOT reproduced, NOT scoped).** Reported second-hand and the specifics were not available, so **the exact surface is UNCONFIRMED — fix nothing until it is**; the candidate fixes differ in size and one has a real trade-off. **Ask the auditor two things:** (a) which DevTools pane (Application → Local Storage / Application → Cookies / a Network response), and (b) what role they were signed in as, or whether they were signed in at all.
    - **RBAC was read against the code and is CLEAN** — this is the serious version of the finding and it does not hold. `/audit-trails` is `readOnly: true, readRoles: ADMIN_ONLY` (`server/routes/index.ts:136`), `/users` is `readRoles: ADMIN_ONLY` (`:57`), and `password_hash` / `otp_hash` / `reset_token_hash` are all `select: false` in `server/models/User.ts`, so no hash ever reaches a browser. **If the auditor saw audit-trail data as a NON-admin, that contradicts the code and takes priority** — re-test the live endpoint before believing either side.
    - **⚠ REAL, verified independently of the report: `selected-school` outlives logout and carries the previous user's ID.** `AuthContext.tsx:174-176` clears `floral_cached_user` + `has-session` on logout but deliberately KEEPS `SCHOOL_KEY`, which stores `{userId, school}` (`:57`). On a shared clinic PC, DevTools → Local Storage shows the last user's `userId` and school after they logged out. **Do NOT "just clear it" — the trade-off is real:** the `userId` is exactly what stops a second user inheriting the first one's school choice (`loadStoredSchool:43-50`), and keeping it past logout is deliberate and commented, so returning staff aren't re-asked. If it is fixed, the options are `sessionStorage` or storing a non-identifying hash of the userId.
    - **Two things that LOOK like findings but are not — have these answers ready for defense:** (1) `access_token`/`refresh_token` ARE readable in Application → Cookies, because `httpOnly` blocks JavaScript and not the DevTools viewer; that is true of every cookie-auth site and requires already being at the logged-in user's machine. (2) `floral_cached_user` (id/name/email/role/schools) sits in localStorage while signed in — that is the offline-session feature (`offline/authCache.ts`) working as designed, and it IS cleared on logout.

User wants the IPTR and the chart's prev/next patient buttons to read **surname first**. STUDENT stores one encrypted `full_name` (`server/models/Student.ts:10`), so there is nothing to sort or display by surname; the nav buttons currently show `name.split(' ')[0]`, i.e. the FIRST name (`DentalChart.tsx:665,677`). Given the choice between a display-only split, a data-entry convention, and real fields, **the user chose real fields** — the display-only split was rejected because it mis-handles Filipino multi-word surnames (De Guzman, Dela Cruz) and suffixes (Jr., III). Scope, none of it started:
    - `STUDENT` schema + the `fieldEncryptionOptions` list (`Student.ts:34`) — the new name fields are patient PII and must be encrypted like `full_name` is. **Remember the Sprint 26 random-IV rule: plaintext equality queries on encrypted fields never match**, so any name lookup must fetch + filter in JS, exactly as `seedStudents`/`seedRpcVisit2` already do.
    - **Migration for existing records** — split each stored `full_name`; decide what happens to names that do not split cleanly rather than guessing silently. Must use findById+save, not findByIdAndUpdate (encryption).
    - **Chapter 3 ERD + `/docs/DATA-MODEL.md` must be updated** — CLAUDE.md forbids deviating from the ERD, so this is a documented schema change, not a quiet one. That doc is authoritative for field specs.
    - Display/sort sites: DentalChart header + prev/next buttons, PatientList, DentalChartNav/List, TreatmentRecords, RPCTracking, Reports, `useStudents` (`navList` sorts on `a.name`), and the OCR mapping in `iptrOcr.ts` (it extracts a single "name" today and the DOH paper form has separate name boxes — this change actually brings OCR CLOSER to the form).
    - Decide whether `full_name` stays as a derived/compat field or is dropped; dropping it touches every read site at once.

The `patientAge` correction the item raised is also fixed. See both sprint sections above.

Today grade is set per student when that year's IPTR is created, which is fine at demo scale and tedious at rollout. Explicitly a ROLLOUT feature, not a defense one. The user's constraint stands: no per-record prompts or badges across 8,000 records.

    - **Needs its own decision**: `school_id`. A pupil moving between the three schools is a transfer, and there is no transfer flow today.
    - Triaged as fine: address, contact_number, guardian_* are point-in-time and correct on edit; philhealth_status/is_4ps are low-volume annual re-validation.
24. **Unbounded collection reads — APPOINTMENTS HALF DONE 2026-09-02 as Sprint 56; the rest is still open.** Invisible at demo scale (26 students, 6 appointments), real at the Chapter 1 scale of ~8,000 — and "what happens at 8,000 records?" is an obvious panel question.
    - ~~`crudFactory` GET has no limit/pagination on every collection~~ — **partly addressed.** It still has no generic `limit`/`skip`, but it now supports opt-in `filterable` (Sprint 48), `filterableText` and `dateField` (Sprint 56), so a route CAN be bounded. Each model has to opt in; most still have not.
    - ~~Appointments' Completed and Missed tabs have no date bound~~ **FIXED (Sprint 56)** — school-year window + explicit "Show earlier".
    - ~~`useAppointments` pulls /appointments + /students in full~~ **FIXED (Sprint 56)** — date-bounded appointments, then only the referenced students, chunked at 200.
    - ~~APPOINTMENT has no natural boundary to filter on~~ — resolved by bounding on `appointment_datetime` directly rather than inventing a `school_year` field.
    - ~~`useStudents()` pulls SIX whole collections~~ **FIXED 2026-09-02 as Sprint 56b** — joined server-side into `GET /stats/student-rows`; the hook's return shape is unchanged so all eight consumers were untouched. See that section above, including the `.lean()` trap.
    - **STILL OPEN — no generic pagination. INVESTIGATED 2026-09-02; recommended against, and the **user accepted 2026-09-02 — NOT being built.** Reopen only if name search is dropped or a blind index is deliberately adopted.** Two findings, both measured rather than assumed:
      - ⚠ **Server-side pagination is incompatible with the search these screens actually offer.** PatientList, DentalChartList and DentalChartNav all search by SUBSTRING ON THE STUDENT'S NAME (`formattedName.includes(query)`), and names are encrypted with random IVs (Sprint 26) — the server cannot match them by equality, let alone by substring. A paginating server must also filter; it cannot filter by name. Paging without server-side name search would make the search box search only the current page, which is WORSE than today. Making it possible means a blind index (deterministic HMAC of the surname) — a schema change, a migration, and a deliberate weakening of the encryption's threat model, and even then only exact/prefix matching, never "contains".
      - **The payload no longer justifies it.** Measured against the real endpoint: `/stats/student-rows` is **354 B/row raw, 56.8 B/row gzipped**. At the Chapter 1 scale of ~8,000 students that is **2.7 MB raw but ~444 KB gzipped** — and gzipped is what crosses the wire. Sprint 56b already took the large win here (14–58 MB of six collections down to this), so pagination would trade a real security property for ~444 KB on an internal clinic app.
      - **Defensible answer to "what happens at 8,000 records?"**: the reads are bounded and indexed (Sprint 56), the list row is joined server-side into ~57 gzipped bytes (56b), and the list pages in the browser (Sprint 53). Pagination on the server is not free here because of the encryption, and was measured as not worth it.
    - **Superseded wording, kept for context — no generic pagination.** `/stats/student-rows` deliberately returns every student because three consumers aggregate over the whole population. The list-shaped consumers (PatientList, DentalChartList, TreatmentRecords) would still benefit from server-side paging + search; Sprint 53's pagination is client-side only. This is the "option C" that was scoped out twice for blast radius — it needs its own sprint and a decision about the three aggregate consumers.
    - ~~**STILL OPEN — indexes exist on Appointment and Student only.**~~ **DONE 2026-09-03 as Sprint 91** — nine indexes across eight models, each matching a real query shape; six models deliberately left unindexed and the verifier asserts they stay that way. `npm run verify:indexes`.
    - ~~**STILL OPEN — `AuditTrail` is served by the generic CRUD GET with no filter, no sort and no limit**~~ **DONE 2026-09-03 as Sprint 92** — 90-day window + "Show earlier", the screen's date filter widens the fetch, and the dashboard's all-time chart was relabelled. Original finding follows: `GET /audit-trails` returned THE ENTIRE AUDIT TRAIL (found while auditing query shapes for Sprint 91). It is the fastest-growing collection in the system — every action, every user, three schools, forever — and **an index does not fix an unbounded read**. It needs the Sprint 56 treatment: a date bound plus an explicit "show earlier". This is now the largest remaining unbounded read.
    - Archiving old appointments remains the WRONG fix — soft delete is for records removed in error, and archived records are admin-only to view, which would hide routine history from the dentist.
    - **📏 MEASURED 2026-09-05 against dev (26 students), so the scale claim is a number, not a worry.** One open of the Reports page pulls **~108 KB across 11 whole collections**: students 20.9 KB (26 rows) · tooth-records 25.2 KB (130) · medical-histories 13.0 KB · dental-charts 11.5 KB · dietary 8.6 KB · oral-health 8.7 KB · student-iptrs 7.3 KB · preventive 5.8 KB · risk 5.8 KB · schools 1.1 KB · referrals 0.
      - **That is ~4.1 KB per pupil per page open.** At the Chapter 1 scale of **8,000 pupils that is ~32 MB** — and the demo charts only ~5 teeth per pupil. At a realistic 20-32 teeth the tooth records alone reach ~6 KB/pupil, putting a single Reports open near **60-80 MB**.
      - **The hooks are not equally guilty:** `useDohReportData` 11 whole-collection reads, `useRiskClassification` 9, `useSchoolSummary` 6, `useRPCTracking` 6, `useFhsisData` 4.
      - **⚠ THE FORK, which needs a decision before any code:** (a) **server-side aggregation** — one endpoint per report returning the counts, the way Sprint 56b did for the patient list; correct, ends the problem, and the reports' whole join logic moves to the server. (b) **server-side FILTERS** — make `student-iptrs` filterable by `school_year` and scope the children to the resulting ids; smaller, but at 8,000 pupils the id list itself becomes an 8,000-entry query string, so it does not actually scale. **(a) is the real fix; (b) is a smaller sprint that mostly moves the problem.**
25. **PWA updates on an open tab — BUILT (2026-08-25 hourly + Sprint 42 event-driven) and now VERIFIED END-TO-END 2026-09-02. Only the two ⚠ notes at the end remain live.** Original finding: Cost real debugging time today: after Sprints 34/35 deployed, the live site kept serving the pre-deploy build, the "new version" toast never appeared, and F5 fixed it. Nothing was broken in the code — `UpdateToast.tsx` is correct and mounted (`App.tsx:19`), `/sw.js` serves fine on Vercel (static files win over the SPA rewrite in `vercel.json`). **The gap: `useRegisterSW()` is called with NO options (`UpdateToast.tsx:12`), so there is no periodic update check, and the browser only re-checks `sw.js` on a real page load in scope — SPA route changes never trigger it.** With `registerType: 'prompt'`, a long-open tab therefore runs a stale build indefinitely and is never told.
    - **Diagnostic trap worth remembering:** a stale build looks *partly correct*. The OLD `formatStudentName()` also rendered lists surname-first, so the Students list looked right while the chart nav still showed given names — which initially pointed at the data rather than at caching. If a fix "isn't showing", check the SW before re-reading the code.
    - **Preferred fix (A):** `useRegisterSW({ onRegisteredSW(url, r) { setInterval(() => r.update(), 60 * 60 * 1000) } })` — keeps the deliberate "ask before updating" behaviour (this app has forms staff can be mid-way through) while making updates actually get found. ~3 lines.
    - Option B, `registerType: 'autoUpdate'`, is simpler but drops that protection. **Matters before defense:** anyone who opened the app earlier — adviser, panelist — keeps running an old copy until they hard-refresh.
    - **HOURLY CHECK ADDED 2026-08-25 as option A** (`00b7bd06`): `onRegisteredSW` calls `registration.update()` on `UPDATE_CHECK_INTERVAL_MS`. That closed "never checks", but left an up-to-an-hour window.
    - **EVENT-DRIVEN CHECKS ADDED 2026-08-28 (Sprint 42, `UpdateToast.tsx`, tsc both + build clean).** Triggered by the live-verify session that same day, where the deployed site served a pre-Sprint-37 build until the toast was clicked. The interval alone is the wrong shape for the real failure: the common case is a tab left open in the BACKGROUND across a deploy, and coming back to it is exactly when the user is about to act on stale code. `registration.update()` now also runs on `visibilitychange` → visible, window `focus`, and `online`, throttled by `MIN_CHECK_GAP_MS` (60s) so alt-tabbing doesn't hammer the network; `update()` is `.catch(() => {})` since it rejects while offline and that is expected, not a user-facing error. No listener teardown — `UpdateToast` mounts once at the app root and lives for the tab's lifetime (comment in file says so).
    - ~~⚠ **STILL NOT VERIFIED END-TO-END**~~ **VERIFIED 2026-09-02 — the backgrounded-tab case now PROVEN, which was the whole point of Sprint 42.** Method (repeat it this way, it is faithful and needs no throwaway deploy to main): `npm run build` → `npx vite preview --port 4173` (there is **no `preview` npm script** — call vite directly), open a tab, let the SW take control, then edit a source file (the `<title>` in `index.html` is enough — it changes the precache manifest, so `sw.js`'s hash changes: `4c3cd4cb` → `e6915e87`), rebuild, switch tabs, come back. Evidence captured from an in-page recorder:
      - `window focus` → **`updatefound` 136 ms later** → `waiting: sw.js` → toast on screen, while `document.title` was still the OLD one. **Only ~3 minutes had elapsed, so the hourly interval CANNOT explain it** — an event listener did, which is precisely the Sprint 42 claim.
      - Clicking **Refresh** then reloaded onto the new build: title became the `UPDATETEST` one, the in-page recorder was gone (proving a real reload), `waiting` back to `null`, toast dismissed. So the `controllerchange` workaround in `refresh()` holds too.
      - ⚠ **Testing gotcha that will otherwise produce a false failure:** `lastCheck` is initialised to `Date.now()` at registration and `MIN_CHECK_GAP_MS` is 60 s, so a refocus sooner than a minute after load is *correctly* skipped. Wait past 60 s or the test "fails" for the wrong reason. Note also that focusing the tab to run a query is itself a focus event that resets the throttle.
      - Cleanup done: `index.html` reverted (`git checkout`), `dist` rebuilt clean (0 occurrences of the marker), preview server stopped, test tab closed.
    - ⚠ **`registerType: 'prompt'` is a deliberate choice, not a bug**: staff can be mid-form, so the app asks rather than reloading under them. It follows that a user who ignores or dismisses the toast KEEPS running the old build. No amount of check-frequency work changes that. If a panelist or adviser is to be guaranteed current code, tell them to hard-refresh, or switch to `autoUpdate` for the defense window only.
26. ~~**No separation between dev and production data**~~ - **CLOSED 2026-09-04 as Sprint 126.** The Sprint 112 rehearsal cluster became the dev database; production's URI lives only in Vercel; all 19 scripts announce their target. Original note follows for the reasoning. **(raised 2026-08-25).** There is ONE database: local `.env` `MONGODB_URI` points at `floral-cluster.edqpjtu.mongodb.net/floral`, which is the same database the Vercel app serves (confirmed 2026-08-25 via the surname-first render on the live site). Consequences, stated plainly so nobody rediscovers them the hard way:
    - **Every maintenance script run from a dev machine hits live records.** Today alone: `backupRaw` (read 308 docs), `splitStudentNames --confirm` (wrote 20 students), and a throwaway 2029-2030 school year added + archived while testing the year-delete fix. All intended and backed up first — but there is no safety net if one is not.
    - **`FIELD_ENCRYPTION_SECRET` sits in the same `.env`**, so anything run on a dev machine decrypts patient PII (names, addresses, contacts, guardians). Encryption protects data at rest in Atlas; it restricts nothing running locally.
    - This is also why the parked demo-data purge (item 17) is genuinely risky: its dry-run and its real run target the same database the clinic would use.
    - Direction if ever scoped: a second Atlas database (free tier allows more than one) for dev, with `.env` pointing at it and production's URI living only in Vercel. **Requires its own `FIELD_ENCRYPTION_SECRET` for the dev db — do NOT reuse production's**, and seeded dev data rather than a copy of real records.
    - Related standing warning already in CLAUDE.md: never change `FIELD_ENCRYPTION_SECRET` on a database that holds records, it makes them permanently undecryptable.
    - Backups containing real PII live in `dental-4-12-main/project/backups/` (gitignored) — e.g. `backup-2026-08-25T14-57-54-570Z` taken before the name migration. Keep them off shared drives.

29. **Citable source sent by a classmate 2026-09-02 — Bhatia, Gupta, Kumar, Mahajan et al., "Artificial intelligence based techniques for caries risk prediction and assessment: A scoping review", *Journal of Oral Biology and Craniofacial Research* 15(6), Sept 2025, DOI 10.1016/j.jobcr.2025.08.027, CC BY 4.0.** Read from the ABSTRACT only (screenshot) — get the full text before citing specifics.
    - **Directly supports the algorithm choice**: of 13 included studies, the most-used methods were **logistic regression (n=9) and random forest (n=8)** — both are in Floral's five. Reported sensitivity 0.59–0.996, specificity 0.531–0.943, explicitly heterogeneous.
    - **Supports the honest SVM framing** in `docs/ch2-additions-draft.md`: SVM does NOT appear among the most-used methods, which is consistent with defending it as a comparison baseline rather than an expected winner.
    - ⚠ **One discrepancy worth confronting rather than hiding**: the review's most frequent predictors are **socio-demographic factors, oral hygiene habits and dietary habits** — it does not foreground DMF/dmf, which CLAUDE.md makes Floral's PRIMARY feature. Floral does use dietary habits and oral-health conditions too, so this is a Chapter 4 discussion point, not a contradiction.
    - Manuscript work is paused, so this is logged as a source, not drafted into Chapter 2.

Needs its own rule shape, and the model MUST be added to `schoolScope.ts`'s table or it fails closed and returns nothing at all.

"Can I put notes on a date, if I click a date?" — the Appointments calendar renders month grids (`getAppointmentsForDay`) but a day tile is not clickable and there is no note model. 32. **Photos of students, and of staff accounts (idea, 2026-09-02, NOT scoped) — ⚠ this is the app's FIRST file storage.** Verified 2026-09-02: there is NO file storage anywhere — no multer/GridFS/S3, no upload route, and OCR runs in-browser via Tesseract.js without persisting the image. Adding photos therefore means choosing where bytes live and how they are protected.
    - **Photographs of identifiable minors are the most sensitive data the system would hold** — more so than the encrypted text fields, and unlike them, an image cannot be field-encrypted the way `mongoose-field-encryption` works today.
    - Options with real trade-offs: GridFS in Atlas (stays inside the existing 512 MB free tier — ~8,000 photos at 200 KB is 1.6 GB, so it does NOT fit); object storage (new vendor, new key, turnover item); or per-record base64 (bloats documents, breaks the backup script's shape).
    - Staff account photos are far lower risk than student photos and could ship independently.
    - Needs a decision from the adviser/dentist about consent before it is built: the Appendix C consent form covers dental records, and whether it covers photography is not established here.
